Intrusive Memories: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event.\n  - Distressing Dreams: Recurrent distressing dreams in which the content and/or affect of the dream are related to the traumatic event.\n  - Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks): Dissociative reactions (e.g., flashbacks) in which the individual feels or acts as if the traumatic event were recurring.\n  - Psychological Distress at Cues: Intense or prolonged psychological distress at exposure to internal or external cues that symbolize or resemble an aspect of the traumatic event.\n  - Physiological Reactions to Cues: Marked physiological reactions to internal or external cues that symbolize or resemble an aspect of the traumatic event.\n  - Internal Avoidance: Efforts to avoid distressing memories, thoughts, or feelings about or closely associated with the traumatic event.\n  - External Avoidance: Efforts to avoid external reminders (people, places, conversations, activities, objects, situations) that arouse distressing memories, thoughts, or feelings.\n▸ trauma_related_alterations\n  - Fragmented Memory: Inability to recall only specific parts of the traumatic event while remembering others.\n  - Generalized Amnesia: Inability to recall the majority or the entirety of the traumatic event.\n  - Negative Self-Beliefs: Distorted beliefs such as 'I am fundamentally bad,' 'I am damaged,' or 'I am worthless'.\n  - Negative Beliefs about Others: Distorted beliefs such as 'No one can be trusted' or 'Others are dangerous'.\n  - Negative Worldview: Extreme beliefs such as 'The world is completely dangerous' or 'Life is meaningless'.\n▸ arousal_and_reactivity\n  - Impulsive Anger: Sudden explosions of anger for trivial reasons or no clear reason.\n  - Verbal Aggression: Attacking others with shouting, cursing, or blame without holding back.\n  - Physical Aggression: Physically attacking people or throwing/breaking objects.\n  - Chronic Irritability: A state where nerves are always sharp and sensitive, ready to explode at the slightest touch.\n  - Scanning for Danger: Always scanning the surroundings and being alert to find potential threats or risks.\n  - Safety Checking: Repetitively confirming safety, such as checking exit locations or observing people around.\n  - Sustained Alertness: Unable to relax tension even in safe environments and maintaining a constant state of alertness.\n▸ Critical Diagnostic Criteria\n  - Duration: 5 months\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the profile, set matched=true, list related\n  sections/items in matched_sections, and write a short English strategy in answer_strategy\n  on what tone and content the patient should reply with.\n- If the question is not in the profile or can only be inferred, set matched=false and instruct\n  in answer_strategy to respond briefly (e.g., \"I'm not sure\", \"I haven't thought about it\").\n- If patient_turn_index is 1 (patient's first reply), ONLY focus the strategy on the chief complaint\n  (do NOT reveal other symptoms).\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"matched\": true|false, \"matched_sections\": [\"...\"], \"answer_strategy\": \"...\"}\n"

Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks): Dissociative reactions (e.g., flashbacks) in which the individual feels or acts as if the traumatic event were recurring.\n  - Psychological Distress at Cues: Intense or prolonged psychological distress at exposure to internal or external cues that symbolize or resemble an aspect of the traumatic event.\n  - Physiological Reactions to Cues: Marked physiological reactions to internal or external cues that symbolize or resemble an aspect of the traumatic event.\n  - Internal Avoidance: Efforts to avoid distressing memories, thoughts, or feelings about or closely associated with the traumatic event.\n  - External Avoidance: Efforts to avoid external reminders (people, places, conversations, activities, objects, situations) that arouse distressing memories, thoughts, or feelings.\n▸ trauma_related_alterations\n  - Fragmented Memory: Inability to recall only specific parts of the traumatic event while remembering others.\n  - Generalized Amnesia: Inability to recall the majority or the entirety of the traumatic event.\n  - Negative Self-Beliefs: Distorted beliefs such as 'I am fundamentally bad,' 'I am damaged,' or 'I am worthless'.\n  - Negative Beliefs about Others: Distorted beliefs such as 'No one can be trusted' or 'Others are dangerous'.\n  - Negative Worldview: Extreme beliefs such as 'The world is completely dangerous' or 'Life is meaningless'.\n▸ arousal_and_reactivity\n  - Impulsive Anger: Sudden explosions of anger for trivial reasons or no clear reason.\n  - Verbal Aggression: Attacking others with shouting, cursing, or blame without holding back.\n  - Physical Aggression: Physically attacking people or throwing/breaking objects.\n  - Chronic Irritability: A state where nerves are always sharp and sensitive, ready to explode at the slightest touch.\n  - Scanning for Danger: Always scanning the surroundings and being alert to find potential threats or risks.\n  - Safety Checking: Repetitively confirming safety, such as checking exit locations or observing people around.\n  - I also don't hold any bizarre or irrational beliefs; my thoughts are just tied to the very real distress and fear of what I went through.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe how you've been avoiding people, places, or activities that remind you of the traumatic event, and how this avoidance has been affecting your daily life and relationships?\n\nPatient: I actively go out of my way to avoid the area where it happened and constantly decline social invitations to avoid any conversations about it. This isolation has really strained my relationships with family and friends, and trying to bypass these triggers completely disrupts my daily routine."
  }
]

Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.  Subtypes: Initial Insomnia, Middle Insomnia, Early Morning Awakening, Hypersomnia, Nightmare Disorder, Non-Restorative Sleep\n[S028] Psychomotor_Changes: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.  Subtypes: Psychomotor Agitation, Psychomotor Retardation (Motor), Psychomotor Retardation (Speech)\n[S029] Fatigue: Persistent fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.  Subtypes: Chronic Fatigue, Physical Fatigue, Mental Fatigue, Loss of Energy\n[S030] Worthlessness_Guilt: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick). Subtypes: Reduced Concentration, Indecisiveness, Subjective Memory Complaints, Clouded Thinking\n[S032] Death_Suicide: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.
